export async function goToMenu(page: Page, label: 'Products' | 'Categories') {
  await apiHelper.triggerAndWaitWithRegex(page, page.getByRole('button', { name: label }).first(), /.*~list$/);
}

export async function openCreateDialog(page: Page) {
  await apiHelper.triggerAndWaitWithRegex(page, page.getByRole('button', { name: /Create/ }).first(), /.*template$/);
  await expect(page.getByRole('dialog')).toBeVisible();
}

export async function submitCreateDialog(page: Page) {
  await apiHelper.triggerAndWaitWithRegex(page, page.getByRole('dialog').getByRole('button', { name: /Create$/ }), /.*(list|~create)$/);
}

  if (p.stock !== undefined) await type(/^Stock/, String(p.stock));

[8563] ASSISTANT: Round 16U is finished and committed locally (`54a2db3`, on top of `afd0063`; nothing pushed). Both Playwright projects now pass when run serially (`--workers 1`) against the final build.

## Tests
- **Customer project, full run:** 78 passed, 1 skipped (the old "empty state" skip). My last full run had one failure, a stock assertion in F23. I patched it and re-ran that file with the F49 and Level 8 specs (20 passed), but I did not repeat the whole customer run afterwards.
- **Customer `--grep @smoke`:** 15 passed, run on its own.
- **Admin project:** 35 of 35 passed in the last full admin run. I made two small spec fixes afterwards, to the seed and to the category lookup; the seed spec was re-run alone and passed. I did not repeat the full admin run.
- **Why the admin tests failed before:** the specs expected the 8 seeded products on one page, but the shop now has about 250. I fixed the specs, not the data:
  - They now page to the row they need (a `seek` helper).
  - The seed now checks for existing rows before creating them. It had created a second "Footwear" category and a duplicate product on an earlier run. I removed the duplicate category with one SQL delete; it was my own test artefact and had no products.
  - Typing into the generated dialogs is verified and retried. The category dialog's required "Commission (%)" field takes the first keys when it has focus.
- **Specs that checked exact counts:** the reservation job from F73 cancels old unpaid orders at any time, which changes day counts and stock between two readings. F48, F23 and F49 now check the direction of the change instead of an exact number.
- **Data and Docker:** all data was kept. Docker's disk went read-only during a long run; I restarted Docker Desktop and Postgres recovered (602 orders afterwards).

## Screens converted
- **Customer, cards via the hook (10):** products, orders, returns, return an item, notifications, addresses, quotes, seller products, my sales, exchange rates.
- **Customer, custom views (19):**
  - 8 detail layouts: order, quote, sale, return, wallet, seller shop, company, notification.
  - 11 relation lists as cards: order lines, returns, shipments, reviews, gift cards, points history, quote lines, members, invitations, seller ledger, sale lines.
- **Home page:** the existing custom page now uses the shared product card.
- **Admin (6 screens):** the dashboard with KPI tiles, the order queue ("Orders (30 days)" and "All orders"), the return-request queue with Approve and Reject, the quotes list, and the order, return and quote detail summaries. The quote discount field stays editable.
- **Shared look:** one card, status badges, a toolbar and action bar, and one detail layout across both apps.
- **Layout tweaks:** cards now take two columns on tablets, and the pager is hidden while a list is empty. The top bar fits at 1440px; at 1280px the last group ("Sell") is behind the scroll arrow.

## Screens left generated
- The profile form and its addresses table, because it is an editing form (R16U-12).
- All operation input forms, so validation and error messages stay unchanged.
- The customer shop settings view.
- Admin plain CRUD tables.
- Low stock, because it shares the product table with the product CRUD.

[8608] ASSISTANT: All 15 failed specs now pass: 17 passed, 0 failed, with no retries, run serially in 15.3 minutes. The 17 are the 15 failures plus two extra tests from the files I selected whole (`level5.spec.ts:416` also pulled in the F40 back-office test). Nothing in the code or tests needed to change.

**Why they failed.** The failures were not a Keycloak fault. I couldn't find anything wrong with it, and the real cause is an inference from the Docker state:
- **Keycloak is healthy.** The container is `keycloak-webshop` on port 8280, under `/auth`. It has been up for 8 hours with 0 restarts, no out-of-memory kill and about 460 MB in use. The log has no errors, and the realm discovery endpoint answers in 2–10 ms.
- **Postgres is healthy.** `postgres-webshop` also shows 0 restarts, with 17 connections.
- **The machine was under load.** Other stacks run on the same Docker VM, which has about 23 GB. A litellm stack from another project has exited with code 137 within the last half hour. Docker also went read-only once earlier in this round. The `#kc-form-login` timeouts therefore most likely came from Docker slowing down during the pw-r16u-q run.
- **I changed nothing on the Keycloak container.** I didn't set a memory limit or a restart policy, because both live in the generated compose setup.

**Comparison with pw-r16u-m.** That run had 67 passed and 9 failed, all with data or timing causes: catalog-home, checkout, five in level5, F60 and F68. The 9 failures were fixed in earlier work. Of the 15 failures in pw-r16u-q, only F40 was also in pw-r16u-m, and it passes now.

**Not re-run.** The other tests that passed in pw-r16u-q were not run again. The code has not changed since the last commit, so the last known state is still the full customer run (78 passed, 1 skipped), the admin run (35 of 35) and the smoke run (15 passed), as in the round report.

**Operational notes.**
- Run Playwright with `-c playwright.local.config.ts`. My first attempt without it failed on a missing Chromium build, so that run proved nothing.
- Run it with `--workers=1`, and keep the other Docker stacks idle during full runs.

## Intent

Data that lives in an external system (here: advertising cost) must stay fresh in the application database. A small set of scheduled jobs does this: a frequent job fetches the current state, a daily job resets per-day counters, a daily job closes entities past their end date. The jobs hold no logic; one service does the work, so the same code can also be called from operations.

## Practice

### Platform base

- The agent-docs describe no scheduler. Scheduling is application infrastructure (OSGi components configured through Configuration Admin, see [[quartz-scheduled-job]]). The practice below is therefore a convention buil

[8652] TOOLRESULT: package com.example.webshop.webshop.custom.webshop;

/** F47: one place that normalizes e-mail addresses (best-practices/backend/email-normalization-at-invite.md). */
public final class Emails {
    private Emails() {}

    /** null stays null, otherwise trimmed and lower-case. */
    public static String normalize(String email) {
        return email == null ? null : email.trim().toLowerCase(java.util.Locale.ROOT);
    }

    public static boolean looksValid(String email) {
        return email != null && email.matches("[^@\\s]+@[^@\\s]+\\.[^@\\s]+");
    }
}

 * F36: the one clock of the custom Java code (best-practices/backend/injectable-business-clock.md).
 * Production: the system clock. A demo or test run can start the application with the environment variable
 * WEBSHOP_BUSINESS_DATE (ISO date or date-time) or the system property of the same name: the clock then starts at that
 * moment and keeps advancing. It is read once at class load, so changing it needs a restart: a browser test cannot set it
 * (GAPS R10). Model expressions (`Date!now()`, F37 sale window) use the platform clock, not this one.
 */

     * fixed offset (agent-docs/backend/platform-behavior-reference.md "Time and Time Zones", GAPS R12-4).

## Intent

Email bodies, reports and generated diagrams are text with variable parts. Keep the text in a Handlebars template and fill it from a context object at runtime. The template can change without a code change when it lives in the FileStore.

## Practice

### Platform base and what is a convention

The agent-docs cover only the surroundings: SMTP is configured through `JUDO_PLATFORM_MAIL_SMTP_*` environment values ([email configuration](../../agent-docs/deployment/application-config.md)), the FileStore is a platform service ([[filestore-mediated-file-transfer]]), and custom services are OSGi components. The agent-docs do not describe a Handlebars library or an email-sending API: rendering with Handlebars and the `EmailService` calls below a

---
## Intent

Custom operations send e-mail (activation codes, reports, reminders). Inject a mail service, build the message in a small service, and take sender, server and templates from configuration rather than code.

## Practice

### Inject a mail service and wrap it

Custom operations get OSGi services with `@Reference` (see [Injecting DAOs and Services](../../agent-docs/backend/data-access-guide.md#injecting-daos-and-services)). Most applications use the `EmailService` of the `hu.blackbelt.email.api` module with a builder-style message (from, to, subject, body). The agent-docs do not describe that API, so check the artifact version in your application. One application used a vendor SDK directly instead.

Put the call in one service (`EmailSenderService` in the examples) with a method per mail type. Operations then call the service, and tests replace it with a no-op implementation.

### Take server settings from platform configuration

The platform's own SMTP settings are the `JUDO_PLATFORM_MAIL_SMTP_*` variables: host, port, user, password, auth, STARTTLS, from, plus the transport protocol and SSL trust (see [Email (SMTP)](../../agent-docs/deployment/application-config.md#6-email-smtp)). A vendor SDK needs its own parameters. Custom application parameters can use the OSGi Configuration Mapper or Config Admin (see [Configuration Mapper for Custom Parameters](../../agent-docs/backend/platform-behavior-reference.md#configura

## Intent

Send the platform's transactional emails (invitations, verification links, approval notices and similar) from one backend service. The text of each email is a template that administrators can change in the Configuration singleton, so the wording does not need a deployment. The service keeps the rendering and sending steps in one place, so custom operations call one method per email type.

### Templates live in the database

Each email type has its template text in a configuration field (see [[platform-configuration-singleton]]). The service reads the template on each send and renders it with the values of that send.

### One method per email type

The service exposes a typed method for each email, such as one for invitations and one for verification links. Each method builds its own context and does not expose the raw template engine to callers.

## Structure

This is a backend pattern with no model shape of its own. The service depends on three things: a configuration reader that returns the templates and platform settings, the platform's email sending service, and, for attachments, the file store that holds the documents. The service has no entity and no transfer object. Its context is a plain builder object that carries the values a template may use, such as action URLs, display names and expiry periods.

## Constraints

- A template that is not configured is a skipped send. The service logs it and returns; it does not fail the calle
[8709] TOOLRESULT: - Each email method: (1) loads template from `ConfigurationTemplateService`, (2) builds a context model with dynamic values, (3) renders HTML, (4) sends via `EmailService.sendMessage(EmailMessage.emailBuilder()...)`
- Interceptors do not run around the custom operations that call this service (they fire only on built-in operations), so each caller invokes the send explicitly.
- Graceful degradation: if template is null (not configured in DB), the method logs and returns without error
- Document attachment support: retrieves files from `FileStoreService` by ID and attaches as `BinaryAttachment`

**Template variable conventions:**
- Action URLs (registration, verification, magic link): built from the base URL plus a route and a token parameter.
- Display names: platform name, organization name, user name, partner name, company name.
- Subject and content title.
- Expiration periods, in days or minutes.

Names are listed in prose rather than as template placeholders, so the guidance stays free of template syntax.

**Email context pattern:**
- A dedicated context POJO (e.g., `EmailContext`) with builder pattern, holding all template variables
- Context object is passed to `Handlebars.Context.newContext()` for type-safe rendering

## Examples

### Example (project code)
- Pattern: the email service renders Handlebars templates stored in the database Configuration entity, using `ConfigurationTemplateService` to load templates and platform settings. Sends via JUDO's `EmailService` O

## Intent

Run periodic work (cleanup, expiry, sync, external refresh) outside the request cycle, without putting the business logic in the job class. The job only triggers; a shared service does the work and can be reused by operations.

### Jobs are thin and configured from outside

Each job is an OSGi component that implements `Runnable`. Its schedule and tuning values come from OSGi Configuration Admin, so the cron expression can change without a code change. A job without its configuration does not start.

### One scheduler module, one run at a time

The jobs live together in a dedicated scheduler module, and a job is not allowed to overlap with its own previous run. The module is a backend-only concern; no model element is generated for it.

## Structure

A job is a component that exposes the `Runnable` service and requires its configuration before it activates. It references one shared business service (and through it the generated DAOs). The job body is a short wrapper around a single service call. Configurable values are declared with a typed configuration definition.

Jobs fall into five kinds: data cleanup (delete or transition stale records), processing pipeline (pick up pending records), expiration (move past-due records to an expired state), sync (reconcile related data), and external refresh (pull data from an outside API).

## Constraints

- The job must not let an exception escape `run()`. It logs the error

## Intent

Administrative users need to download data as CSV (for example a compliance export of audit rows) and to upload a CSV that drives a bulk action (for example inviting many e-mail addresses). Both are modeled as custom operations whose file goes through the file store, not through the operation payload.

## Practice

### Platform base

- A binary attribute travels as a file-store token: upload is two-phase (request the upload token, upload the content, send the token as the attribute value in the create or operation input), and invalid tokens fail with `INVALID_FILE_TOKEN` ([binary upload tokens](../../agent-docs/backend/platform-behavior-reference.md#binary-upload-tokens)). An import operation therefore receives a `FileType` input whose id the backend resolves against the file store.
- An operation without an output answers 204; an operation that returns a binary hands back a `FileType` the client downloads with the token ([client side of the upload token flow](../../agent-docs/backend/platform-behavior-reference.md#client-side-of-the-upload-token-flow)).
- The platform also has modeled `EXPORT` operations that call the `Export` SPI (Excel through JXLS); without a configured `Export` they fail with `Not implemented` ([Excel export operations](../../agent-docs/backend/platform-behavior-reference.md#excel-export-operations)). A CSV export is a custom operation, not that SPI.
- Custom operations are OSGi components and bring their own servi

## Intent

Ingest a batch of records from an external JSON file in one operation, and keep a record of which batch produced each imported item. The batch row holds the raw input, the time, and the count of created items. Items refer back to their batch.

### Each import leaves a batch row

The import operation writes an Upload row before it creates items. The row is the audit trail: it keeps the raw JSON, the time of the upload, and how many items were created. Upload is not created through a generated create action.

### Imported items point back to their batch

Each created item holds a reference to its Upload. Tracing an item to its source batch is a single navigation. Items that already exist are skipped, so a batch can be re-run without duplicating records.

## Structure

The Upload entity has three required attributes: json (text), timestampOfUpload (timestamp) and nrOfUploadedQuestions (integer). Upload has a 0..* association to Question. Question has a required text identifier, an optional back-reference to Upload (0..1) and an optional Category (0..1). Category has a required name identifier.

The import and download operations are not part of the entity shape. They are static operations on a transfer object (an application choice) that take the JSON as an unmapped transfer object input and return a file reference or the export payload.

## Constraints

- Upload rows are created only by the import operation. Create, update and delete flag

140-- No platform scheduler. `judo-services-job` only runs initializers at startup; no Quartz/scheduling component. Periodic work = app code (e.g. OSGi component with own executor).
141-- Scheduler thread: no principal, no `ACTOR`.
142-- Use `Dispatcher.callOperation` for a modeled op (gets transaction/operation handling) instead of DAO. Script/custom ops run in own transaction.
143-- Don't rely on `ACTOR` access or variables unless you put an actor in the exchange.

    /**
     * Moves the order to {@code to}. {@code customer} = the caller is the shop customer: the order must be theirs and
     * only NEW orders can be cancelled. Cancelling gives the stock of every line back.
     */
    public void transition(Serializable orderId, OrderStatus to, String trackingNumber, boolean customer) throws ShopFaultException {
        // entity re-fetch: the bound transfer object is a copy (best-practices/backend/entity-re-fetch-pattern.md)
        SalesOrder order = salesOrderDao.getById(orderId,
                SalesOrderMask.salesOrderMask().withNumber().withStatus().withTrackingNumber()).orElseThrow();

        if (customer) {
            assertCustomerOwns(order);
        }
        OrderStatus from = order.getStatus();
        if (!allowedFrom(to, customer).contains(from)) {
            throw fault("INVALID_TRANSITION", "Order " + order.getNumber() + " is " + label(from).toLowerCase()
                    + ": it cannot be set to " + label(to).toLowerCase() + ".");
        }

        if ((to == OrderStatus.SHIPPED || to == OrderStatus.DELIVERED) && salesOrderDao.countSubOrders(order) > 0) {
            // F63/F64: an order with seller shipments is shipped and delivered by the sellers; its status follows
            throw fault("SHIPPED_BY_SELLERS", "Order " + order.getNumber() + " is shipped by its sellers: the status follows their shipments.");
        }
        if (to == OrderStatus.CANCELLED) {
            giveStockBack(order);

[8840] TOOLRESULT: - Adding a stored member to a fresh/new DB: no manual step; generated Liquibase changelog (`application/model/target/generated-resources/model/<app>-liquibase_postgresql.changelog.xml`) already creates the column.
- Existing DB is NOT migrated automatically. Startup fails `There are RDBMS model changes which have not been applied to database`. Migrate or discard DB+volume. Never use `ignoreVersionCheck` to get past it.
- Manual schema-upgrade needed for persistent DB with data and for non-additive changes (renames, type changes, removals).
- Local persistent PostgreSQL: `./judo.sh schema-upgrade` (generates diff vs running DB and applies).
- `judo.sh` schema module path = `schema_dir` (default `model_dir`). Set `schema_dir=<dir>` in `judo.properties` or env, e.g. `schema_dir=application ./judo.sh schema-upgrade`.
- `DATABASE HAVE TO BE UPDATED!` logged at error level by `generate` = notice, not failure; build still `BUILD SUCCESS`; migration needed before new model runs on that DB.
- Migration REQUIRED when entity types change: add/remove/modify mapped entities; attributes on entities; attribute types; relationships; entity inheritance.
- NOT required for: transfer object types, actors/access points, UI layouts/forms, operations (unless they affect entity structure), validations, derived attributes. Reason: schema generated only from entity types.
- Module: `application/schema/`; CLI tool + Docker image; auto script generation; incremental any-to-any; manual SQL support; Post

/**
 * F73: the work of the scheduled job. A modeled static operation, so that the dispatcher gives it a transaction
 * ({@link com.example.webshop.webshop.custom.webshop.ReservationJob} calls it; agent-docs/backend/platform-behavior-reference.md
 * "Scheduled Work": the scheduler thread has no transaction and no ACTOR).
 */
@Component(immediate = true, service = com.example.webshop.webshop.operation.webshop.shopjobs.ReleaseExpired.class)
public class ReleaseExpiredCustomImplementation implements com.example.webshop.webshop.operation.webshop.shopjobs.ReleaseExpired {
    @Reference ReservationService reservations;
    @Override
    public ShopMessage get() throws ShopFaultException {
        int released = reservations.releaseExpired(BusinessClock.now());
        return ShopMessage.builder().withMessage(released + " order(s) released").build();
    }
}

 * F21-F24: turn the cart of the logged-in customer into an order.
 * One transaction (the dispatcher runs every operation in one): stock is decremented line by line, the order
 * lines copy name and unit price (snapshot), the order gets a number from the SalesOrder sequence, the cart is

// Row cards of the customer app. Each one is a few lines: which field is the title, the subtitle, the badge and the
// meta rows. The labels come from the generated column headers, so they follow the language switch.

type Row = Record<string, any>;
type Ctx = {
  row: Row;
  label: (field: string) => string;
  date: (v: unknown) => string;
  t: (k: string, d: string) => string;
};

const labelOf = (columns: GridColDef[], field: string) => columns.find((c) => c.field === field)?.headerName ?? field;

const useCtx = (row: Row, columns: GridColDef[]): Ctx => {
  const { t, i18n } = useTranslation();
  return {
    row,
    label: (f) => labelOf(columns, f),
    date: (v) => (v ? new Date(v as any).toLocaleString(i18n.language || 'en-US', { hour12: false }) : ''),
    t: (k, d) => t(k, { defaultValue: d }) as string,
  };
};

[8994] TOOLRESULT: import type { ChipProps } from '@mui/material/Chip';

type Color = ChipProps['color'];

// One colour per kind of state, for every status label of the shop (orders, returns, quotes, sub-orders, products).
// The labels are server strings (English or Hungarian), so the map looks at the beginning of the text.
const RULES: Array<[RegExp, Color]> = [
  [/^(new|új|requested|kért|open|nyitott|draft|piszkozat)/i, 'info'],
  [/^(awaiting|jóváhagyásra|pending|függőben|waiting|várakozik|unpublished|nem publikált)/i, 'warning'],
  [
    /^(paid|fizetve|kifizetett|approved|jóváhagyva|accepted|elfogadva|published|publikált|delivered|kézbesítve|refunded|visszatérítve|done)/i,
    'success',
  ],
  [/^(shipped|kiszállítva|feladva|in transit|packed)/i, 'primary'],
  [/^(cancel|lemondva|rejected|elutasítva|declined|expired|lejárt|failed|sikertelen)/i, 'error'],
];

export const statusColor = (label?: string | null): Color => {
  const text = (label ?? '').trim();
  return RULES.find(([re]) => re.test(text))?.[1] ?? 'default';
};

export const statusBadge = (label?: string | null) => (label ? { label, color: statusColor(label) } : null);

[9014] TOOLRESULT: /** Setting "Payment provider": the whole shop pays through this provider. */
async function setProvider(admin: { page: Page }, label: string) {
  await openSettings(admin.page);
  const box = admin.page.getByRole('combobox', { name: /Payment provider/ });
  if ((await box.inputValue()) !== label) {
    await box.click();
    await admin.page.getByRole('option', { name: label, exact: true }).click();
    await expect(box).toHaveValue(label);
    await saveView(admin.page);
  }
  await btn(admin.page, /Back/).click();
}

async function closeOutput(page: Page) {
  await page.getByRole('dialog').getByRole('button', { name: /Back/ }).click();
  await expect(page.getByRole('dialog')).toHaveCount(0);
}

}

[9020] TOOLRESULT: // The generated dialog can lose focus while the address is typed; stray digits then land in the points field. Reset it explicitly.
async function resetPoints(d: ReturnType<Page['locator']>, points?: string) {
  const box = d.getByRole('textbox', { name: /^Loyalty points to spend/ });
  await box.click();
  await box.fill(points ?? '');
  await box.blur();
  await d.page().waitForTimeout(700); // DebouncedTextField
  if (points !== undefined) await expect(box).toHaveValue(points);
}

// A checkout that must be refused: the readable message shows (it is a toast, so look for it at once), no order opens.
async function checkoutRefused(page: Page, extra: { giftCard?: string; points?: string }, message: RegExp, shotName: string, shotId: string) {
  await openCart(page);
  await btn(page, /Check out/).click();
  const d = page.getByRole('dialog');
  await expect(d).toBeVisible();
  await typeInto(page, d, /Shipping address/, 'Lake Road 9, 5003 Bergen, Norway');
  if (extra.giftCard) await typeInto(page, d, /^Gift card code/, extra.giftCard);
  await resetPoints(d, extra.points);
  await d.getByRole('button', { name: /(Ok|Submit)$/ }).click();
  await expect(page.getByText(message)).toBeVisible({ timeout: 15000 });
  await shots(page, shotId, shotName);
  await expect(field(page, /^Number/)).toHaveCount(0); // nothing was ordered
}

[9044] TOOLRESULT: 
Access getters on an `ActorType` are strict; violating any rule below yields an obscure JQL-parser or ASM-validator error. All four rules apply together.

1. **The filter base must be the entity FQN**, not the TO:

    ```text
    <model>::entities::<Entity>!filter(x | …)      // ✅ ASM-valid
    <model>::services::<TO>!filter(x | …)          // ❌ ReferenceExpressionMatchesBinding
    ```

    The generator projects from the entity to the target TO automatically.

2. **`self` is not in scope.** The actor has no `self`; always iterate from the entity with a lambda variable. This applies to actor **access getters** only. A derived member on a transfer object or entity (including the principal's transfer object) does have `self`, which is the instance it is evaluated on; see [advanced-modeling-patterns.md](../model/advanced-modeling-patterns.md).

3. **`accessType="DERIVED"` requires a reference-returning expression.** A boolean literal (`getterExpression="true"`) is rejected by `ReferenceBindingExpressionIsValid`. For an unrestricted "see everything" access, drop the getter and set `accessType="ALL"`.

4. **`!getVariable(...)` returns a scalar `String` only.** Compare with `==`, not `!memberOf(...)`:

    ```text
    e.<path> == <model>::types::String!getVariable('PRINCIPAL', 'partner_code')
    ```

    Primitives must be fully qualified; bare `String!getVariable(...)` fails with `Unknown symbol: String`. Multi-value claims (`Sequence<String>!getVariable(...)` does not exist i

181-| Variable | Purpose |

183-| `JUDO_PLATFORM_MAIL_SMTP_HOST` / `_PORT` | SMTP server |
184-| `JUDO_PLATFORM_MAIL_SMTP_USER` / `_PASSWORD` | Credentials |
185-| `JUDO_PLATFORM_MAIL_SMTP_AUTH` | Enable authentication |
186-| `JUDO_PLATFORM_MAIL_SMTP_STARTTLS_ENABLE` | Enable STARTTLS |
187-| `JUDO_PLATFORM_MAIL_SMTP_FROM` | Sender address |
188-| `JUDO_PLATFORM_MAIL_TRANSPORT_PROTOCOL` | Transport protocol |
189-| `JUDO_PLATFORM_MAIL_SMTP_SSL_TRUST` | Trusted SSL hosts |
190-| `JUDO_PLATFORM_LOG_SMTP_SERVER` | Log SMTP interactions (`true` in the shipped files) |
191-
192-**Mail defaults:** without configuration the SMTP port is `25`; with `logSmtpServer=true` (`JUDO_PLATFORM_LOG_SMTP_SERVER`) the default of `mailSmtpPort` becomes `10025` instead. The local log SMTP server itself listens on `logSmtpServerPort`, which defaults to `1025` (host `logSmtpServerHost`, default `localhost`).
193-
194-**Local testing with Mailpit:** run a mail catcher such as Mailpit locally; the app only knows an SMTP host and port, so for local runs point them at `localhost:1025` (no authentication, no TLS) in `judo-karaf.env`. # Default user name for SMTP.
# String
mail.smtp.user = ${mailSmtpUser!((logSmtpServer!"true")?switch("true", mailSmtpUser!"user", ""))}

# Default password for SMTP.
# String
mail.smtp.password = ${mailSmtpPassword!((logSmtpServer!"true")?switch("true", mailSmtpPassword!"password", ""))}

# The SMTP server to connect to.
# String
mail.smtp.host = ${mailSmtpHost!((logSmtpServer!"true")?switch("true", "localhost", "localhost"))}

# The SMTP server port to connect to, if the connect() method doesn't explicitly specify one. Defaults to 25.
# int
mail.smtp.port = ${mailSmtpPort!((logSmtpServer!"true")?switch("true", "10025", "25"))}
